Plans 3, 4 and 5 are written, built and merged: #145 (Home and keypad money flows), #146 (jar pages and goal ring), #147 (Learn quest map and daily check-in). No migrations. Worktrees are removed; local main is still at the old commit, so it needs a pull.

**Tests:** last full run, at the top of the stack before the review fixes, was 91 suites / 783 tests passing, core 1035 passing, lint and typecheck clean. After the CodeRabbit fixes, those touched tests passed locally and every push went through the hook that runs `pnpm test`. #145 CI `verify` passed; #146 and #147 CI were still running when you merged, and I didn't check their result.

**Not verified:** nothing was run on a device. The Maestro flows were updated to the new labels but never run. Worth checking on a phone first:
- the flow-sheet footer stays above the keyboard;
- the quest map spacing;
- the + tab label.

**Problems found on the way:**
- **Jest hang:** mobile Jest finished all tests but never exited under `pnpm test`, so pushes stalled at the pre-push hook. Cause: React Query's 5-minute cleanup timers in test clients, a leak main already had but which stayed hidden when Jest ran in parallel. Fixed in `apps/mobile/tests/setup.ts`, plus one test that pressed a button twice in overlapping steps and left Jarso's blink timer running.
- **Force-push blocked:** after the CodeRabbit fixes, the force-push of the jars and learn branches was blocked. I merged the base branch upward instead and pushed normally; I saved that to memory.

**CodeRabbit on #145:** all three comments held up and are fixed:
- the "What happened?" sheet now scrolls;
- the avatar and bell have larger tap areas;
- the Spend tile is now teal-700 (see the rulings).

**Rulings I made** (cost if wrong in brackets):
1. Home bell opens a new `/(child)/news` page, because `/notifications` is already the push-settings page. [route change]
2. The + sheet allows 36 words instead of 32, to keep the mockup copy word for word. [trim copy]
3. Money refusals shown to the child are calm blue, not red, although the plan said to reuse the red `Notice`. [none]
4. Home shows at most 2 moment cards, to stay within 30 words. [a third card waits until one clears]
5. Spend tile is teal-700, not the spec's teal-600: `docs/design.md` only allows white text on teal-700 or darker. [colour tweak]
6. Spend week sentence describes this week only ("₹30 went out of Spend this week") instead of comparing with last week. [copy tweak]
7. Goal stars and goal-reached show no Sparks or badge chips, because the server sometimes pays 0. [less reward feedback]
8. Removed the Home "next step" card; it could swallow the goal-reached celebration. [none]
9. Answer sheet sits under the question, not docked at the bottom, so it's visible on long questions. [layout rework]
10. Dropped answer pictures that gave the right answer away (saving piggy, calendar). [fewer pictures]
11. Learn chest shows existing badges and looks, not a new reward, because a new one would need a migration. [later migration]
12. A failed daily check-in is lost, not queued. It is asked once per family day. [an occasional missed check-in]
13. Icons on purple use plain "white", following the existing purple button; no token stays white in both themes. [token swap]
14. Process: small pure-logic tasks were batched for review, a few one-line fixes I checked myself without a re-review, and the Jarso and Learn whole-branch reviews ran before the final wiring. [the final review caught the gaps either way]

**Left open:**
- The `.claude-logs/` session file is uncommitted; it needs its own logs PR.
- Leftover minors: a requests↔home type-only import cycle in core, `graphCopy` and `chooseHomeStep` no longer used by the phone, and two older tests on main that press a button twice in overlapping steps.

Five plans are left, numbered as in the roadmap at the top of plan 1 (`2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-1-foundation.md`):

6. **Diary, Jarso tab, Me, the bell, level-up and customising Jarso.**
   - The Diary and Jarso tabs get the new look.
   - Me opens from the avatar.
   - A level-up celebration.
   - The child picks Jarso's lid colour and accessory, using the level unlocks that already exist.
7. **Pocket money split into Spend and Save.** Parents set how each payment divides between the jars. This needs:
   - an ADR (number 0019);
   - a migration;
   - changes to the shared code and the parent's allowance screen.
8. **Child onboarding (about 2 minutes, skippable).**
   - Meet Jarso.
   - Practice coins to try Spend and Save; they're labelled PRACTICE and never touch real money.
   - Pick a goal.
   - Make Jarso yours.
   - Replay from Me.
9. **Parent sign-up and onboarding on the phone.**
   - Sign-up with a 6-digit email code.
   - Seven steps, including "Your child calls you" and freedom presets that map onto the existing rules.
   - A QR card to hand the account to the child.
10. **Parent restyle.**
    - Family cards where asks are answered in one tap.
    - Handover cards.
    - The new look, calmer than the child side.

Plans 6, 8 and 10 can run in parallel the same way 3, 4 and 5 did. They need no migrations, just separate file ownership. Plans 7 and 9 change the backend: 7 adds a migration and 9 changes the auth email template. Both need your approval before anything goes to the hosted Supabase project, so they're better done one at a time.

5. Child shell: tabs Home · Learn · + · Diary · Jarso, the raised gold `PlusTabButton`, the `/(child)/add` sheet, and the inbox moved out of the tabs.
6. Home rebuild: header, Jarso hero, moment cards, `JarTile`, `GoalCard`, `NextStepCard`. The Home test moves from `tests/features/jars/` to `tests/features/home/`.
7. "I bought something" and "I got money" as keypad flows, celebrating with `celebrate()`. Undo stays on the purchase.
8. Save some / Use savings / Ask for extra: same hooks and payloads, `JarsoGuess`, `celebrate()` instead of `SentToast`.
9. My asks becomes a tracker; the push card shows on My asks after the first ask ("Want to hear when Mum answers?").
10. `pnpm check`, docs updates (`mobile.md`, `design.md`), then stop so the controller opens the PR.

Existing tests that will break are covered in the plan: `tabs.test`, the Home test (moved), `purchase-sheet.test`, the "Got money" tests in `diary-more.test`, `requests/sheets.test`, `request-list.test`, `push-permission-card.test`, one href each in `news.test` and `taps.test`, and two cases in `goals.test`.

Spec or contract ambiguities I resolved:
- **Bell target:** it opens a new page `/(child)/news`, not `/(child)/notifications` as the contract says. That route is already the push-settings page that Me opens. `/news` keeps the old inbox URL and doesn't clash with the parent's `/inbox`.
- **+ tab file:** it is `(tabs)/plus.tsx`, not `(tabs)/add.tsx`. Route groups don't change URLs, so `add` would clash with the `/(child)/add` sheet.
- **PredictStep:** kept unchanged because the goal sheet (plan 4's file) still uses it. The new `JarsoGuess` export sits in the same file.
- **Picture grids:** they use core's category keys and labels (Apps, A gift…), not the mockup's labels (Books, Gifts). Icons and tints map to them in core.
- **Pocket-money-day card:** no "Got it from Mum" button, because only the parent confirms a handover. No split is shown either; the split is plan 7.
- **Save some:** keypad plus quick chips like every other flow, instead of the mockup's coin slider.
- **Use savings / Ask for extra:** two steps (amount, then a "For what?" grid) as the spec describes, not one screen as in mockup F6. The picked reason becomes the existing free-text `reason`.
- **Rule lines:** they are dropped from the sheets. Only the limit shows, as one chip. Refusal messages stay word for word.
- **Celebration chips:** worked out from the change in progress before and after the action, so daily caps are respected. The mockup's "Spend now ₹75" card is left out because the `Celebration` type has no card slot.
- **Home blocks:** `HomeStepCard` (owned by plan 4) leaves Home, and the goal-reached moment is left to plan 4. `PendingPurchases` stays on Home because it only shows when purchases are waiting on the phone.
- **Guess wording:** "allowance(s) closer" (core's unit), not the mockup's "weeks". No guess is shown when the outcome is "same".
- **Word-budget check on Home:** the goal and mission cards are left out of the count, since plans 4 and 5 own their text.
- **Small edits outside plan 3's files**, all listed in the plan: the inbox route in `notice-listener.tsx` and `use-push-taps.ts`, removing `SentToast` from `sheet-parts.tsx` (it has no other user), and the two predict cases at the end of `goals.test.tsx`. `goals.test.tsx` is the one plan 4 may also edit, so expect a rebase conflict there.

I wrote the plan with 9 tasks. It is the only file I changed, and it is not committed. I ran none of the plan's tests; the commands in it are for the person who implements it.

Plan: `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/jars/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-4-jars.md`

**Tasks:**
1. Core `weeklyTotals`: turns the existing `balanceHistory` into 6 weeks, Monday to Sunday on the family's local dates, with bar labels ("Sep 28", "12", …, "This week"). Weeks can be negative, and weeks before the history starts count as 0.
2. Core week line (`weekLine`), `isBestWeek`, `jarPageFill` (reuses `jarsoFill`) and `jarPageCopy` (titles, captions, and the two actions per jar with their routes).
3. Core goal stars and weeks: `starsLit`, `starToCheer` (reuses `MILESTONES` and `milestoneCrossed`), `weeksToGoal`, `toGoLine` ("₹110 to go · about 4 weeks"), and new `goalCopy.page` text.
4. Mobile: move `useBalanceHistory` out of `jar-graph.tsx` into its own file, and add `WeeklyBars` (number on each bar, calm stub for a negative week, "Best week!" chip, one screen-reader summary).
5. Mobile: `JarScreen` plus the route `app/(child)/jar/[jar].tsx`. It shows the big Jarso, the bars, one sentence and the two keycap actions; Spend links to `/(child)/purchase` and `/(child)/received`, Save to `/(child)/put-in` and `/(child)/take-out`. Any other jar name redirects.
6. Mobile: `GoalRing`, `MilestoneStars`, `useSavingPace` and `useGoalMoments`. The last one fires a star cheer with a +25 chip, or the full goal-reached `celebrate()` with "Ask Mum to buy it" leading to take-out, then marks the goal celebrated.
7. Mobile: goal page per mockup J2, and `GoalCard` restyled to ring + bar + "₹X to go" with the same props. Deletes `goal-staircase.tsx` and updates `goals.test.tsx`.
8. Full `pnpm check`.
9. Integration after rebase onto `feat/mobile-kid-home`: add `jar/[jar]` to PAGES in `(child)/_layout.tsx`, pass `onPress` to Home's `JarTile`s, delete `jar-graph.tsx` (and `JarsCard` if Home no longer uses it), add Home tile tests, re-run the targeted tests.

`JarGraph` has no user in the parent app. Today only Home (plan 3's file) imports it, so deleting it waits for Task 9.

**Spec ambiguities and my choices:**
- **What a bar shows:** how much the jar's balance changed that week, taken from the balance history as the spec says. Save's heading is "Saved each week". Spend's is "Each week", with neutral words and no "!".
- **Spend jar page:** the mockup's "Spent each week" with top category icons is dropped. It would need purchase-level data, and the movements the balance history reads don't say what kind of entry each one is.
- **Negative week:** a muted stub labelled "−₹30", never red, never counted as best week. The sentence is "You used some savings."
- **Week line copy:** Save going up gives "₹50 more than last week!", a quieter week gives "Every coin counts. Keep going!", and a first-time child gives "Your first save shows here." Spend lines are neutral and never cheer.
- **"About N weeks":** worked out from the goal's plan and the allowance frequency (weekly, monthly, quarterly or annual) as `ceil(remaining × 52 / (plan × periods per year))`. It is hidden when there is no plan or the allowance is stopped.
- **"Ask Mum to buy them":** written as `Ask ${useParentLabel()} to buy it`, because goal names can be singular. The button opens `/(child)/take-out`.
- **When stars cheer:** the count already cheered is stored per goal in AsyncStorage and only ever goes up. The first time a goal is seen, the stars are lit quietly with no cheer. When several stars light at once, one cheer for the highest. The 4th star is left to the goal-reached celebration. Nothing cheers when money leaves or the target is raised.
- **Where goal moments fire:** in both `GoalCard` and `GoalScreen`, so Home gets them without any Home edits. A check per goal id and a cached `celebratedAt` write stop them firing twice.
- **The 90-day history window:** kept as is, because `JarGraph` shares that cache until Task 9 and 90 days already covers the 6 weeks.
- **Big jar:** Jarso drawn in each jar's colour on both pages. Save uses the same fill as Jarso on Home; Spend uses the same formula with the jars swapped.
- **Action names:** defined for now in core `jarPageCopy`. If plan 3 exports the same names from core, Task 9 switches to those.
- **`HomeStepCard`:** left in place. It may simply stop being rendered once plan 3's Home lands.

I wrote the plan to `/home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.claude/worktrees/learn/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-5-learn.md`. Nothing is committed and no other file was touched. It has not been run yet. I checked the class names it uses against the Tailwind config and the scripts it calls against `package.json`.

The plan has the header, Goal / Architecture / Tech Stack / Spec, Global Constraints with the contract rules copied in, file ownership, a rule that freezes the mission player's behaviour, and 5 Review Focus edge cases.

Tasks:
1. **Core quest map** (`learning/quest.ts`, `quest-copy.ts`): 7 stops per week on a winding path, Jarso on the current stop, chest at the end, plus the chest reward (badge and look only). Vitest.
2. **Core picture art** (`learning/option-art.ts`): a keyword-to-emoji picture for each answer option, with a letter tile when nothing matches. Vitest.
3. **Core check-in rule** (`progress/checkin.ts`): `checkinDecision` returns show, skip or refresh, using family-local days. Includes a test for just before and after midnight in Kolkata.
4. **Quest map on Learn**: `QuestMap` component, reworked Learn screen (streak and Sparks pills, week banner), restyled `NextStepCard` with the same props, and the e2e `learn.yaml` selector updated.
5. **Questions**: picture tiles, a `JarsoHint` bubble, the prompt as one big heading, Hint and Check keycaps, a grape progress bar. Only the render code changes; a diff check proves the frozen functions are untouched.
6. **Answer sheets**: green "Nailed it!" with a pop sound and confetti; calm blue "Close! Try again"; a calm blue "Here's how" after the second wrong try. A test checks the sheet has no red. The finish card gets Jarso cheering. `learn.test.tsx` only swaps its heading strings.
7. **Daily check-in**: `features/checkin` (screen, an AsyncStorage marker for "asked today", `useCheckinPrompt`) and the `app/(child)/checkin.tsx` route. Nope and Yes both go through `useDailyCheckin`; Yes opens `/(child)/purchase`; failures are silent.
8. **Docs and `pnpm check`**: updates `docs/mobile.md` and `docs/design.md`, then the full check and grep guards.
9. **Integration after the rebase onto `feat/mobile-kid-jars`**: add `checkin` to SHEETS in `(child)/_layout.tsx`, call `useCheckinPrompt()` on Home, re-run the targeted tests and typecheck.

Spec ambiguities I resolved:
- **Today's stop:** missions have no day gate, so "today's stop" is the current (first unfinished) mission. When everything is done, Jarso stands by the open chest.
- **Weeks:** the content has exactly 7 missions, so there is one week today. The layout still splits into chunks of 7 and handles fewer than 7. Week titles come from the current mission's title, because the content has none.
- **Chest reward:** no "week complete" badge exists, and adding one needs a migration. The chest shows `missions_5` (week 1) or `missions_10` (week 2), plus the best look unlocked at the child's level. It gives nothing new and never money.
- **Picture answers:** the content has no pictures. A core keyword map adds emoji, which the spec allows for lesson category art. No content or schema changes, so the web is unaffected.
- **One-line prompt:** the full prompt is shown as a big heading, not cut short. Content stays as it is.
- **Copy:** `playerCopy`, `roadmapCopy` and `learnCopy` are shared with the web and stay untouched. New phone strings go in `questCopy`.
- **Check-in data:** progress has `checkedInToday` but no last check-in day. The rule combines the time progress was last read, a per-user "asked today" marker in AsyncStorage, and `familyToday(timezone)`. It asks for a fresh read when the cached read is from an earlier family day.
- **Check-in offline:** a failed check-in is lost, not queued. Queuing it would need a new server-side outbox kind, which is backend work this plan must not do.
- **Once per day:** the marker is written when the sheet opens, not when it is answered, so a failure or a swipe-away never asks again that day.
- **Check-in day strip:** the mockup's 7-day strip is replaced by a streak count plus the "1 free rest day a week" line, because progress has no per-day data for it.
- **Right-answer sound:** `playSound("pop")`. The existing success haptic stays in `check()` so it doesn't fire twice. The wrong answer has no sound.
- **Placement:** the answer sheet sits inside the player, not in a Modal and not via `celebrate()`, so the player's state and the existing tests keep working.
- **Naming:** the new route is `/(child)/checkin`. The existing `learn/check-in` is the unrelated one-time placement quiz.
- **One file outside the listed ownership:** the check-in rule goes in a new `packages/core/src/progress/checkin.ts` plus one line in the progress barrel, so it sits next to plan 2's check-in copy. No other plan owns that folder.

The table gives sub-hints "From Spend", "Gift, pay, found", "Give cash to Mum" and "Ask Mum for it". The implementer invented different strings: "Write down a purchase", "Add money you received", "Move money into Save" and "Ask to take money out". The brief left hints unspecified, but the spec fixes them, so the implementer's own choice does not stand.
  - The Save hints carry the parent's name ("Mum", falling back to "your parent"). The spec table writes those two as "Give cash to Mum" and "Ask Mum for it", so as plain strings the Save hints cannot be right. They need a `parent` parameter, as `caption.save` already has. The brief's `hint: string` type must change for the Save pair. For example, make `actions.save` a function of `parent`, or make `hint` `string | ((parent: string) => string)`.
  - The test at `packages/core/tests/ledger/weekly.test.ts` (the `jarPageCopy` describe) checks only labels and hrefs, so it would not catch wrong hints.
  - `starToCheer` never returning the 4th star matches the brief. It is documented, and the `1→4` jump cheering 0.75 is consistent with the contract. Verified at `milestones.ts` lines 20-24 and the "leaves the fourth star" test.
  - Everything else is present, file by file:
    - T1: `weekly.ts`, the `ledger/index.ts` export and the tests.
    - T2: `weekLine`, `isBestWeek`, `jarPageFill` and the other `jarPageCopy` fields.
    - T3: `milestones.ts`, the goals barrel export, `goalCopy.page` and `card.toGo`.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff: Plan 3 may export the action names from core, and Task 9 repoints `jarPageCopy.actions` at them. The controller should confirm the hint strings are reconciled there, or fix them now.

### Strengths
- `weeklyTotals` follows the brief closely. It uses a Map lookup with no second walk-back, `min(Sunday, today)` for the end date, and UTC labels.
- Dates use the shared `addDays` and `startOfWeek` helpers.
- `starToCheer` reuses `milestoneCrossed`. The `weeksToGoal` formula equals `paymentsLeft` for weekly plans, and a test pins that.
- `jarPageFill` is a single formula, swapped for Spend.
- Tests are behavioral and cover the briefed edge cases: Monday boundary, empty history, pre-history weeks, negative weeks, the 8-word cap and no "!" for spend.
- Core purity is respected.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- `packages/core/src/ledger/copy.ts` (diff lines 99-107): replace the hints with the spec's sub-hints and make the Save hints parent-aware, as described above. Add test assertions for the hints.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `weekly.ts` `weekLine`, Save branch:
  - `cur > prev` fires even when `cur <= 0`. With prev -500 and cur 0, a week with nothing saved reads "₹5 more than last week!". That is odd for a child.
  - Guard the branch with `cur > 0 && cur > prev`. The fallthrough then gives "Every coin counts" for cur = 0, and "You used some savings." for cur < 0.
  - No test covers the negative-to-less-negative case.
- `weekly.ts` `weekLine`, Spend branch: Spend bars are balance changes, so spending makes them negative. Spending ₹30 this week after ₹10 last week gives cur -3000 < prev -1000, and the line says "₹20 less than last week." That is the reverse of what the child did, which is more spending. Decide whether the sentence should compare magnitudes or net change. The spec's example is Save-oriented, so confirm intent before the screen task.
- `weekly.ts` `balanceOn`: any date missing from the map falls back to the first point's balance. That is correct only for dates before the history starts. If the history ends before `today`, or has gaps, the result is a bogus large delta. This is fine if `balanceHistory` is dense through today, which I did not check. Clamping to the last point for later dates would be safer.
- `jarPageCopy.caption.spend` and `barsLabel` have no test.
- The report says the RED run was not captured. This is process only.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The core logic and tests are sound and match the briefs. The action hint strings do not match the spec's money-action table, and the Save hints need the parent's name, so `jarPageCopy.actions` has to change before the screen task consumes it.

chars: 3385

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant for Tasks 1-3. Every listed file has its hunk, including both barrels. Interfaces, layout rule, status mapping, Jarso placement, chest-open rule, chest badge/wear, and checkinDecision branch order match the briefs. Kolkata midnight test traces correctly (now 00:01 Nov 7 local, readAt 23:59 Nov 6 -> refresh).
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: `familyToday`, `localDateOf`, `availableWear` signatures and `availableWear(1)` ending in "cap" / `availableWear(5)` ending in "crown". The report says typecheck and the suites passed. I did not re-run them.

### Strengths
- `quest.ts` is pure, with relative imports and no platform globals. Every x is clamped. Short weeks, empty journeys and the all-done case are handled.
- `ChestReward` has a type-level shape of badge, earned and wear, so money cannot be added without a test failing.
- `checkinDecision` is a small pure function, and its family-local day handling is correct.
- Tests assert behavior: widths 280/324/400, clamp bounds, `C` count, 7+1 and 9-done week splits.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor
- `quest.ts:77-79` (brief-mandated geometry): Jarso beside a stop can overlap it. Stop 3 is at x=259.2 at width 324. Jarso's x is clamped to 292, only 33px from the centre, under the 64px stop size. The same happens for lanes ≥ ~0.7 and worse at narrow widths. Flip Jarso to the other side when the clamp bites, or check it in the Plan 5 map component.
- `quest.ts:105-114` / `CHEST_BADGES` (brief-mandated): the chest badges `missions_5` and `missions_10` unlock at 5 and 10 total missions. So week 1's `earned` can be true while `chest.open` is false (5 of 7 done). Week 2 onward all map to `missions_10`. The consumer UI must combine `earned` with `chest.open` and must not show "earned" from `ChestReward.earned` alone.
- `quest.ts:112`: `wear` depends only on level, not on the week, so every chest shows the same look.
- `option-art.ts:12-21`: `save|saving` -> 🐷 and `nothing` -> 🤷 can hint the right answer in saving-themed questions. The match is unanchored, so "saved", "filmed" and "unsaved" match. Use `\bsav` and check that no two options in one step get the same emoji.
- `option-art.test.ts:20-30`: the "every shipped option gets art without throwing" test is near-vacuous, because `optionArt` cannot throw and `seen > 0` is the only real assertion. Assert a match rate or distinct art per step. The brief mandated the wording.
- `checkin.test.ts:60-68`: the London case skips because `lastPromptDay === today`, not because of the timezone. Use `lastPromptDay: "2026-11-05"` and `checkedInToday: false` so it expects "show" and depends on the London day.
- `quest.test.ts:112`: the local `const first` shadows the module helper `first`.
- `quest.test.ts:70-77`: the bound `32` is hard-coded to the default stopSize/2.
- Process: no captured RED for tasks 1 and 3, per the report. Only task 2 has one.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the briefs and the binding constraints. The quest map has a winding 7-stop week with Jarso on today's stop and a chest that gives a badge and a look, never money. The check-in decision uses family-local days only. The findings are minor, mostly UI-consumer hazards (Jarso overlap, chest earned versus open) and test-strength polish.

### Finding Verdicts

- **`jarPageCopy.actions` hints must be spec sub-hints; save pair takes parent's name; hints asserted** — ADDRESSED. `packages/core/src/ledger/copy.ts`: spend hints are "From Spend" and "Gift, pay, found". `save` is now `(parent) => [...]` with "Give cash to ${parent}" and "Ask ${parent} for it". `packages/core/tests/ledger/weekly.test.ts` (jarPageCopy block) asserts the spend hints and `save("Mum")` hints.
- **`weekLine` Save: "more than last week!" only when `cur > 0 && cur > prev`; cur = 0 gives "Every coin counts"; cur < 0 gives "You used some savings."; test prev −500 / cur 0** — ADDRESSED. `packages/core/src/ledger/weekly.ts` weekLine: the guard is now `cur > 0 && cur > prev`. cur = 0 with prev = −500 skips the "more" branch, fails `cur === prev` and fails `cur < 0`, so it returns "Every coin counts. Keep going!". cur < 0 still returns "You used some savings." because the `cur < 0` check comes before the fallthrough. New test "does not cheer a save week that only climbed back to zero" covers prev −500 / cur 0.
- **`weekLine` Spend: unambiguous net-change sentence, neutral, no "!", ≤ 8 words; test −3000 vs −1000** — ADDRESSED. The lines are now "Spend grew £X more than last week." and "Spend dropped £X more than last week.". Each is 7 words, has no "!" and doesn't cheer. The wording says what the jar did, so the old "less" for more spending is gone. The new test checks −1000→−3000 gives "dropped £20" and −3000→−1000 gives "grew £20".
- **`balanceOn`: dates after the last history point use the last point's balance; test where history ends before today** — ADDRESSED. `weekly.ts` now has `const last = history.points.at(-1)`, and `balanceOn` returns `date < first.date ? first[jar] : last[jar]` when there is no exact match. The new test "a history that ends before today holds its last balance" ends history on 11-03 (TODAY is 11-06) and expects 700. The old code would have returned 100 for today and a total of 0, so the test fails without the fix.

### New Breakage in the Fix Diff

None. Details:
- The Spend wording "Spend dropped £20 more than last week" is a little awkward. It reads as "a £20 bigger drop than last week", which is what the net-change bars show. Minor wording, not blocking.
- I did not grep for other consumers of `jarPageCopy.actions.save`, because the grep failed on a zsh glob error. The report puts Tasks 1–3 in `packages/core` only, so a mobile consumer is unlikely this early. The change from array to function would break any consumer that indexes it directly.
- The report names `pnpm exec vitest run tests/ledger tests/goals` (108 pass), typecheck and biome clean. This is consistent with the diff, and I did not re-run it.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- The report notes an unrelated failure in `packages/core/tests/offline/submit.test.ts`, which this fix does not touch.
- The first-round report says tests were written alongside the implementation, with no separate RED run captured.

### Verdict

**Fix round:** All findings addressed, no new Critical/Important breakage.

timestamp: 2026-11-06T06:25:53.802Z
time: 2026-11-06 06:25
chars: 4666

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every file the three briefs list has its hunk: `core/package.json` export, `home/{index,keypad,pictures,words,hero,moments,copy,sparks,guess}.ts`, `diary/copy.ts` (append only), `requests/flow-copy.ts`, the `requests/index.ts` barrel line, and all listed tests. Signatures, strings, icons, tints and the money-action table match the briefs and the spec.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff alone:
  - That Biome accepts the `requests/flow-copy.ts` runtime import of `@jarso/core/home`. The report says Biome is clean.
  - That the Sparks values are not duplicated. `sparkChips` only diffs `Progress`, so it is fine, but the numbers live in `@jarso/core/progress` and I did not check them.
- ⚠️ Process: the report admits tests and implementation were written together, with no RED run. That departs from the brief's TDD steps. It is not a code defect.

### Strengths
- `pressKey` (`keypad.ts:333`) is small and total. The "every result parses" property test (`keypad.test.ts:879`) is seeded and deterministic.
- `heroLine` reuses `balanceHistory` and `jarChange`, so it stays consistent with the web graph caption. Every branch has a test, plus word-budget and shame-word checks.
- `selectMoments` follows the ordering, cap and exclusion rules. It does not mutate its input, because `filter` copies before `sort`.
- Web strings are untouched: the diary copy is append-only and `requests/index.ts` only adds one export.
- `capitalise` is used only at sentence starts, with tests for "your parent". The `reasonText` 140-character cap and the empty-Other-fails-the-schema case are tested against the real schemas.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- `requests/flow-copy.ts:583-610`, `askCelebration`'s fallbacks can state something false.
  - `status` is typed `string`, so the `over_available` and `over_cap` results from `types.ts:45-54` compile and are accepted.
  - A deposit with any non-`pending` status returns `` `${m} in Save!` `` (line 595). An `over_available` deposit would therefore tell the child the money is in Save when nothing was sent.
  - A withdrawal that falls through gets "Asked Mum!" with `tracked: true` (line 607), which links to a tracker for a request that does not exist.
  - Callers are said to handle `over_*` first, but the type does not enforce it, and the table test (`flow-copy.test.ts:1124`) only covers the success statuses.
  - Fix: narrow `status` to `"pending" | "approved" | "confirmed" | "done" | "already_sent"`. The test table would then be exhaustive. Alternatively, make the fallback branches explicit and map unknown statuses to a neutral or untracked result.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `home/guess.ts:238,247`, the lead on the fallback line.
  - "Yes! A bit closer." and "Good guess! A bit further." read fine and stay within 8 words.
  - Keeping the lead on both branches is consistent and I would keep it.
  - The test only covers the lead on the fallback in the right-guess case (`guess.test.ts:783`). Add a wrong-guess fallback assertion.
- `home/moments.ts:375`, `localeCompare` on ISO strings assumes a consistent timezone format.
  - Mixed offsets would order wrongly. Supabase returns uniform UTC, so this is low risk.
  - There is also no tiebreak on equal `occurredAt`, so ordering of ties is input-dependent.
- `requests/flow-copy.ts:566` and `home/pictures.ts:414` define the same tint cycle twice.
  - Export one constant from `pictures.ts` and reuse it.
  - The `AskReason` icons and labels duplicate `SPEND_ICONS` for the shared keys. Keeping them separate is acceptable because the labels differ ("A gift", "Games").
- `requests/flow-copy.ts:509` and `home/moments.ts:365` form a requests↔home import cycle. It is type-only in one direction, so it is safe at runtime. Home importing `SavingsRequest` is the only reason the cycle exists. It is fragile if either side later adds a value import.
- `home/copy.ts:187,194` and `flow-copy.ts` interpolate `parent` lowercase mid-sentence ("Give cash to your parent"). This is correct. The `moneyActions` tests only check `"your parent"` for word counts, not the strings, which is fine.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved with one fix (the Important item above) recommended before the UI tasks build on `askCelebration`.

**Reasoning:** The pure core is faithful to the briefs and well tested, and the shared web copy is intact. The one real risk is the untyped `status` with lying fallbacks in `askCelebration`. The implementer's two flagged decisions are fine on the merits, except that the over-statuses fallthrough should be made type-safe rather than silently accepted.

- Deletion of `roadmap.tsx` is safe for mobile. Mobile imports no `Roadmap`: the `./roadmap` export is removed from `learning/index.ts`, and `roadmapCopy` is core and still used. The only `<Roadmap` user is `apps/web/src/app/(app)/child/learn/page.tsx:39`, which is the web's own component, not the mobile file. `tasks/todo.md:98` and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-03-mobile-wave-2-design.md:105` still name `roadmap.tsx`. That is stale doc text only. The brief permitted deletion on this grep condition.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - Whether Jarso, flipped left of a stop, overlaps the neighbouring stop or the path. `jarsoCenterX` (quest-map.tsx:~33) considers only the anchor stop. Check against `questMap` geometry, or look at it on a device.
  - Whether `questCopy.today(3)` renders "Day 3!". The new `e2e/learn.yaml` regex `Day [0-9]+!` depends on it, and the flow was not run.
  - Whether the wear keys in core are plain words. `wearName` capitalises the raw key, so a snake_case key would show as "Party_hat".

### Strengths
- Chest-open logic is correct, and the "earned but still shut" case is tested.
- `jarsoCenterX` is a pure exported helper tested at 3 widths. The rendered far-right-lane test asserts there is no overlap with the stop.
- `NextStepCard` keeps its public contract, so Home needs no change.
- The e2e flow keeps the old branches and gates the generic branch on `visible`.
- Colours come from tokens and classes, apart from the white noted below.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important
- apps/mobile/src/features/learning/quest-map.tsx:191 and next-step-card.tsx:36: raw `"#ffffff"` for the icon colours. The binding constraint is "colours from tokens only, no raw hex". Use a token (for example `tokens["primary-foreground"]` or a white palette entry if one exists). I found no existing token precedent for white. `button.tsx` and `switch.tsx` also use `#ffffff`, which is a code smell rather than an exemption.

#### Minor
- learn-screen.tsx:~49-62: the pills' `accessibilityLabel` is the bare number ("5", "240"), so a screen reader gets no meaning. Use short labels such as "5 day streak" and "240 Sparks". This needs copy in core. The tests find them by `getByLabelText("5")`, so they would need updating.
- Report deviation: no RED run (tests written alongside the code and green first time). Tests do assert real behaviour, so I am not escalating.
- quest-map.tsx `mapWidthOf` derives the width as `chest.x * 2`, which is fragile if core changes the chest anchor. Prefer passing `width` explicitly, or exposing it on `QuestWeek`.
- learn-screen.tsx:~76: the banner uses `bg-grape-600`, `text-grape-100` and `text-white` classes. These are token classes, so they are fine.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes (one small item)

**Reasoning:** Behaviour matches the brief and the Jarso x-clamp and chest-open carry-overs are handled and tested. Replace the two raw `#ffffff` icon colours to meet the tokens-only constraint, then approve. The `roadmap.tsx` deletion is safe.

**Finding Verdicts**

- **Raw `"#ffffff"` icon colours in quest-map.tsx and next-step-card.tsx** — ADDRESSED. `quest-map.tsx:27` defines `ON_GRAPE = "white"` and the play icon uses it for both `color` and `fill` (about line 190). `next-step-card.tsx:7` does the same for the `GraduationCapIcon`. A grep for `#fff` over `features/learning` and `features/learn` finds nothing. The round-1 `ai-foreground` token is gone. The new constants cite the `keycap-grape` precedent in a comment.
- **Learn header pills had bare-number accessibilityLabels** — ADDRESSED. `learn-screen.tsx` now uses `questCopy.streak(stats.streak)` and `questCopy.sparks(stats.sparks)`. `quest-copy.ts` defines them as "N day streak" and "N Sparks". `learn-screen.test.tsx:100-101` asserts `questCopy.streak(5)` and `questCopy.sparks(240)`. `questCopy` is imported in that test (line 1).
- **`questCopy.today(n)` must render "Day N!", and `wearName` must be readable for every wear key** — ADDRESSED. `quest-copy.ts` has `today: (day) => \`Day ${day}!\``, and the quest-map test asserts `questCopy.today(3)`. `Wear` in `packages/core/src/mascot/jarso.ts:9` is `none | cap | shades | headphones | crown`. Each is one lowercase word, so `wearName` gives Cap, Shades, Headphones and Crown. Round 1 changed no code for this. The `today(3)` e2e regex match was only verified by reading `quest-copy.ts`. I did not open `e2e/learn.yaml`, and my grep for `today(` in it matched nothing, as expected for a YAML file.
- **Map width derived from `chest.x * 2`** — ADDRESSED. `QuestMap` takes a required `width: mapWidth` prop. `mapWidthOf` is deleted, and a grep of `apps/mobile/src` finds no remaining `mapWidthOf`. `LearnScreen` computes `mapWidth = width - MAP_MARGIN` once and passes it to both `questMap(...)` and `<QuestMap width={mapWidth}>`. ### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every file the brief lists has a hunk, `PredictStep` and its props are untouched, and all the interfaces match the brief.
- ✅ Keypad: `text-money-xl` amount line with label and `accessibilityValue` (`amount-keypad.tsx:81-88`). Live-region error, limit chip, quick chips that set the amount (`:113-116`), and a 4×3 `keycap-white` pad with `h-16` keys. Delete is labelled "Delete" and haptics errors are swallowed (`:62`).
- ✅ Grid, shell, tracker: radiogroup with radio tiles and a teal checked border (`picture-grid.tsx:416-435`). `FlowShell` shows back or X, step dots, a `header` title and a pinned footer. The tracker labels done, now and next, and announces `now` via a polite live region (`ask-tracker.tsx:171-178`).
- ✅ `SentCelebration` is ref-guarded, and `useSparkChips` has a 1.5 s race and never throws.
- ✅ Colours come from tokens. No raw hex and no `"white"` literal.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - Safe-area handling. `FlowShell` adds no top or bottom inset (`flow-shell.tsx:251,294`), yet the test mocks safe-area-context. Check that the consuming screen or modal supplies insets.
  - `sparkChips(undefined, after)` behaviour when `snapshot()` was never called. It is core code from earlier tasks.
  - Whether `Button` dims `disabled` pills (see the first Minor item).

### Strengths

- The kit is small, with one responsibility per file and a barrel.
- Accessibility is thorough: radio `checked` state, "Step n of m", "Now:/Done:/Next:" labels, "Point" and "Delete" labels.
- `JarsoGuess` calls its hooks before the early returns and keys the answer to the amount, so a changed amount re-asks without an effect. A wrong guess uses calm `bg-secondary`, never destructive red.
- `useSparkChips` clears its timer in `finally`. `Promise.race` handles the late rejection, so there is no unhandled rejection.
- The tests exercise real behaviour (a stateful keypad host, the real `CelebrationProvider`, rerender-once for the celebration). The `change_plan` test guards `goal-sheet`.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

1. **Flagged item: selected pill at disabled opacity** (`predict-step.tsx:638-642`). I judge this a real but small defect, and I would fix it rather than accept it.
   - The chosen answer is the one the child just tapped, and it renders dimmest. That reads as "unavailable" instead of "your pick".
   - Fix: drop `disabled`. Guard in `onPress` with `if (guess === null) setAnswered(...)`, and keep `accessibilityState={{ selected }}`. Alternatively, if `Button` supports it, override the opacity on the selected pill.
   - Matching `PredictStep` is not a reason to keep it, since `PredictStep` is another plan's component.
   - I did not open `button.tsx`, so the dimming itself is taken from the implementer's note. It follows from `disabled` on `Button`.
2. `amount-keypad.tsx:55-59`: the doc comment says fractions keep their zeros ("2.50"), but `/\.?0+$/` turns "2.50" into "2.5". The chip label becomes "₹2.5" and `onChange` receives "2.5". Behaviour is acceptable, but the comment is wrong. Either fix the comment or use `/\.00$/` to strip only a zero fraction.
3. `picture-grid.tsx:408,412`: the `columns?: 3` prop is declared and never used. Remove it or implement it.
4. `ask-tracker.tsx:168`: `key={step.title}` can collide if two steps share a title. Use the index or `state`.
5. `use-spark-chips.ts:514`: `before.current` is never cleared after `chips()`. A later `chips()` without a new `snapshot()` diffs against stale data. Clear it after use.
6. Missing test coverage:
   - the 1.5 s timeout path of `useSparkChips`;
   - `SentCelebration`'s `onShown` being called;
   - the `JarsoGuess` mood change.
7. `kit.test.tsx:849`: the inline `require("@tanstack/react-query")` is unnecessary, because `QueryClient` is already imported from that package at `:670`. Import `QueryClientProvider` at the top instead.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief and the global constraints, with sound accessibility and no correctness bugs found. Everything above is Minor; the Further/Closer pill dimming is the only one worth fixing before moving on.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every file in both briefs has its hunk, and the binding constraints hold. I checked them against the diff and made no other repo-wide checks.
  - **Task 4 files:** `use-balance-history.ts` is a verbatim move that keeps `keys.balance` and the 90-day window. `jar-graph.tsx` imports the hook. `weekly-bars.tsx` and the `index.ts` exports are there.
  - **Task 5 files:** `jar-screen.tsx`, the `jar/[jar].tsx` route and the `index.ts` export are there.
  - **Page content:** `JarScreen` shows the big jar with `jarPageFill` and the balance, with gold text for Save. The caption uses `useParentLabel()`. It has a bars card, one sentence bubble, and two keycaps. The Save pair is `keycap-gold` plus `keycap-white`. The Spend pair is `keycap` plus `keycap-white`.
  - **Actions and hints:** the hrefs and hints come from `jarPageCopy.actions` (`packages/core/src/ledger/copy.ts:32-38`). That gives "From Spend", "Gift, pay, found", "Give cash to {parent}" and "Ask {parent} for it". They are set as `accessibilityHint`, and the visible buttons show only the label.
  - **Mood:** `jar-screen.tsx:98` gives `happy` for Spend and for a negative Save week, and `cheer` otherwise.
  - **Failure states:** a failed jars load shows the calm retry through `toUserMessage` (`jar-screen.tsx:27-32`). When history loads or fails, no bars or sentence render, and the jar and actions stay (`:122`).
  - **Constraints:** there is no line graph. I found no edits to Home, `(child)/_layout.tsx` or `(tabs)/**`. Cross-module imports go through barrels.
  - **Route:** the unknown-jar redirect and the `Stack.Screen` title are as specified.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - `tokens.primary` should be the Spend teal and `tokens.gold` the Save gold (`weekly-bars.tsx:26`).
  - `jarPageCopy`, `weekLine` and `isBestWeek` match their Task 1–2 contracts.
  - `Button` accepts the `keycap-gold` and `keycap-white` variants.
  - The controller should confirm all three, since typecheck passing is the implementer's claim.

### Strengths
- `JarScreen` is split into a loader and `JarPage` (`jar-screen.tsx:17`, `:53`), so the history hook never runs on placeholder context. That protects the `keys.balance` cache shared with `JarGraph`.
- Bars are plain Views with one accessible summary and the drawing hidden (`weekly-bars.tsx:38-45`). Negative and zero weeks are a muted stub that is never red. The `MIN_SHARE` floor and the `Math.max(1, …)` divisor rule out NaN heights, and a test covers it.
- The Kolkata Monday 00:30 test checks real timezone behaviour through `localDate` and `weeklyTotals`. The screen tests mock only the data layer.
- The error test asserts that no raw message leaks.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **Pull-to-refresh leaves the bars stale** (`jar-screen.tsx:46-47`, `:106`, `use-balance-history.ts:40`).
  - Pull-to-refresh refetches only `useHome`. `keys.balance` is never invalidated anywhere in `src`, and the hook returns no `refetch`.
  - After a pull, `jars` is fresh but `movements` is stale. `balanceHistory` walks back from the new balance, so a movement made elsewhere (for example a parent approving a request) skews the week totals. The inconsistency is silent.
  - Judgement: this should be fixed, and it is cheap. Return `refetch: movements.refetch` from the hook. That is additive and does not break the brief's `{ data, error }` contract. Then call both refetches from `onRefresh`, or `invalidateQueries({ queryKey: keys.balance(id) })`.
  - `JarGraph` has the same pre-existing gap, so it is not a regression, but the jar page is where a refresh gesture is offered.
- **The jars↔goals barrel cycle is benign today but fragile.**
  - The chain is `features/jars/index.ts` → `jar-screen.tsx:14` → `@/features/goals` → `goal-screen.tsx:4`, which imports `@/features/jars`. `goal-sheet.tsx:15` also imports it.
  - It works now because every cross-barrel symbol is read only inside function bodies. With Babel/Metro CJS interop, the consumer holds the partial exports object and reads the names later, from either entry point.
  - It breaks if anyone uses a `useHome` or `useGoal` binding at module top level, or reorders the `export *` lines. That would give an `undefined` import with no error.
  - The brief mandated the `useGoal` barrel import, so I would not block on this. It is a deliberate trade-off.
  - Cheap hardening: have `JarPage` take the goal target as a prop from a small hook, or move `useHome`, which many features share, into a leaf module such as `features/home-data`. Otherwise record the cycle in `docs/mobile.md` and add a lint or test guard.
- **`weekLine` copy for Spend** reads "Spend dropped £5 more than last week." when the spend week is bigger. This is core's wording (`weekLine`), not this diff's, so the controller may want to review the wording in core. Net-change bars make the phrase ambiguous for a child.
- **No recorded RED run.** The implementer wrote the code before running the tests, which departs from TDD. The tests assert real behaviour, so I am not raising it as a defect.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Tasks 4 and 5 meet the briefs and the binding constraints. The tests check real behaviour, including the timezone case, the calm failure states and the unknown-route redirect. The two flagged items are both Minor. The cycle is lazy-use only and is the mandated structure. The missing history refetch is a small, low-risk fix worth making, for example in Task 9.

They touch only the render block, `ProgressDots`, the `line` derivation and the `CoachBubble` removal. I saw no change to `save`, `check`, `send`, `continueOn`, `busy.current` or the `CurrentStepContext.Provider` value.
  - Whether `border-primary` is the teal border and `tint-teal`, `tint-grape` and `grape-700` exist as Tailwind tokens. Typecheck and tests reportedly pass.

### Strengths

- The frozen player logic is untouched, and the `CurrentStepContext` value is identical.
- `PictureOptions` keeps radio semantics: `radiogroup` with the `pickOne` label, per-tile `radio` role, label and checked/disabled state. The art is hidden from accessibility, so the label comes only from the text. `disabled` is passed to `Pressable`.
- `option-art.ts` is tightened: whole-word anchors, and the `save`/`nothing`/`weeks` entries are gone because they gave away the answer. `optionArts` turns any picture shared by two options into letter tiles.
- The core test now checks every option in every shipped step (more than 30) for distinct art. I ran a throwaway scan in `packages/core` and deleted it afterwards. It found no shipped choice/predict/reason step that mixes emoji and letter tiles, so art cannot single out an option.
- The progress bar keeps the same progressbar role, label and `accessibilityValue`, with `now` clamped.
- New and updated tests cover the three required `step-views` cases and the `JarsoHint` cases.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. `mission-player.tsx`, in the action row: the Hint button now sits inside `!outcome ? (...)`. The old code showed it when `!outcome?.final`. The brief says "same labels/conditions". After a wrong, non-final answer, the child can no longer ask for a hint on the feedback screen. They must press "Try again" first. That is the moment a hint matters most. Fix: render the Hint button in the non-final outcome branch too, or restructure so the Hint/Check row condition is `!outcome?.final`. No test covers this.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. `option-art.ts` and `step-views.test.tsx`: the brief said "art from optionArt(text, i)". The code uses `optionArts` and drops the `weeks` art, so this goes beyond the brief. The reasons are sound, and it is flagged in the report.
2. `mission-player.tsx`, the `<View accessibilityLiveRegion="polite">` wrapper: Feedback now sits below the hint bubble and below the options. On a long step the child may need to scroll to see it. Worth checking in the Task 6 sheet work.
3. `picture-options.tsx`: the fallback `?? { kind: "letter", letter: "A" }` is unreachable, because `arts` has the same length as `options`. It is harmless.
4. `jarso-hint.test.tsx` "Jarso is decorative": it only asserts that there is no `image` role. That is weak, but it matches the brief's wording.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The render rework, accessible radio tiles and the answer-leak fix in option art are solid. The one real regression is the Hint button no longer showing after a wrong try, which the "same conditions" constraint forbids. The fix is small.

### Spec Compliance
- ✅ Spec compliant, with one placement deviation (judged below).
- Frozen-function check: I ran the brief's `git diff -U0 25897848..b9c5299c -- mission-player.tsx | grep -E '…'` command. It printed nothing, so save/check/send/continueOn/busy/CurrentStepContext are untouched. The diff's `StepView` hunks only swap the render blocks.
- Right sheet (`answer-sheet.tsx:61-110`):
  - It uses `bg-success-soft`, a top radius of 24, and Jarso with `moodFor("answerRight")` (cheer) at size 96.
  - The header is `questCopy.sheet.right` in `text-success-text`, with the feedback and predict outcome below it.
  - `Confetti count={16}`, `keycap-success` `xl` and a full-width Continue button are all there.
  - `playSound("pop")` fires once in an effect keyed on `[correct]`.
- Wrong sheets:
  - Not final: `bg-tint-cyan`, think mood, "Close! Try again", the topic link, and a `keycap` Try again button with no sound.
  - Final: heading `questCopy.sheet.shown`, then `answerLine`, the predict outcome and Continue.
  - No red or destructive classes appear in the file.
- The live region wraps the heading and feedback (`answer-sheet.tsx:72`).
- Hint stays after a non-final wrong answer. `hintButton()` renders right under the sheet at `mission-player.tsx:676`, and `!outcome?.final` hides it once the step is settled. The `learn.test.tsx` assertion still covers this.
- Finish card (`mission-player.tsx` `DoneBadge`):
  - Jarso cheer 120 plus `<Confetti />` for mission and practice.
  - The calm badge stays for topic and check-in.
  - The done button is `keycap-grape`.
  - `SavedOnDevice` and the error block are untouched.
- Reduced motion: `Confetti` returns null under `useReducedMotion` (`confetti.tsx:63`), and the test covers it.
- Tests:
  - All six brief tests exist.
  - `learn.test.tsx` has the heading-string updates and both new mocks.
  - Every other assertion is unchanged.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff: whether `check()` always sets `final: true` when `correct`. If it didn't, `buttonLabel` (`answer-sheet.tsx:60`) would show "Try again" on a right sheet. The old code branched the same way on `outcome.final`, so this is probably fine.

### Strengths
- `AnswerSheet` is a small self-contained component that matches the contract interface exactly.
- The `Feedback` duplication is gone from `mission-player.tsx`.
- `PrimaryButton`'s variant type is narrowed to what is actually used.
- The "never red" test checks both the rendered tree and the source file.

### Issues

#### Critical
None.

#### Important
None.

#### Minor
- **Placement under the prompt, not docked at the bottom:** I judge this acceptable and arguably better for this app.
  - The brief and the mockup say bottom-docked. But `StepView` is a scrolling column with options of unbounded height, so a docked sheet would be off-screen on long steps. The global constraint "sheet visible without scrolling on long steps" is met this way, and docking would need a layout rework outside this task's scope.
  - It costs three things:
    - (a) The options jump down when the sheet appears and jump back on Try again.
    - (b) The Hint button sits under the sheet, but the `JarsoHint` text it reveals renders below the options (`mission-player.tsx` ~l.689), so on a long step the hint can land off-screen.
    - (c) The sheet no longer reads as a bottom sheet, yet it keeps the top-only radius of 24 (`answer-sheet.tsx:68`), so a full-width block with rounded top corners and a square bottom looks odd.
  - Fix: either round all four corners, or leave it and record the deviation in the plan or spec.
- **Confetti is clipped:** `answer-sheet.tsx:68` and `mission-player.tsx:343` use `overflow-hidden`, and `Confetti` is `absoluteFill`. The pieces fall over the window height but show only inside the card or sheet. This is fine for the sheet, but the finish card's confetti will look cramped.
- **TDD:** the report admits there was no clean RED. This is process only, and the tests now assert real behavior.
- **Test regex:** `/destructive|red/` in `answer-sheet.test.tsx` is loose and could false-positive on future words such as "colored". It passes today.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The sheet and finish card match the brief, the frozen mission-player logic is verified untouched, and the tests cover the sound, reduced-motion and no-red constraints. The under-prompt placement is a defensible visibility trade-off. The two layout side effects are Minor and don't block the task.

  - `AddSheet` renders the header with an X, a 2-column grid with the odd fifth tile full width, and each tile is a button named by its label with its hint under it. The My asks row shows `n waiting` for pending or approved asks only. `{parent}` comes from `useParentLabel()`.
  - `news.tsx` is moved, `plus.tsx` renders `null`, and both inbox hrefs (`notice-listener.tsx`, `use-push-taps.ts`) are updated along with their tests.
  - A grep of `app src tests` finds no leftover `(tabs)/news` or `tab-news`.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - Whether the add sheet's tile labels are 1–3 words. They come from `moneyActions` in core (Task 2), which is not in this diff.
  - Whether `tabBarButton: () => …` hides the `plus` tab's `title: ""` label under the real js-tabs. The test mocks `Tabs.Screen`, so this needs a device check. The button also ignores the props js-tabs passes, which is fine because it never selects the tab.
  - The mockup copy count behind the 35–36 vs 32 word budget. The controller should decide between loosening the brief and trimming copy.

### Strengths
- The tabs test checks real behaviour: tab order by `testID`, and a press on the rendered `tabBarButton` that asserts `push("/(child)/add")`. The Jarso-hidden cases are kept.
- `AddSheet` takes callbacks and keeps routing out of the component. The route owns `replace`, and `onClose` falls back to `homeFor("child")` when it can't go back.
- The odd-tile full-width rule is computed (`all.length % 2 === 1`), not hard-coded to index 4.
- The close button is 44 dp and the tiles have a `min-h-28` touch target.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- `apps/mobile/src/features/flows/index.ts:1` and `add-sheet.tsx:5`: `AddSheet` does not belong in the flows barrel, and the `jest.mock` in `kit.test.tsx` hides a real coupling problem.
  - What I checked: whether anything imports `@/features/flows` today (only `app/(child)/add.tsx`) and whether `requests`, `goals`, `jars` or `progress` import flows (none do). There is no cycle today.
  - The flows barrel is the leaf UI kit: keypad, picture grid, tracker, shell, celebration. `AddSheet` makes that leaf depend on the `requests` composite, which in turn pulls in `goals` and `jars`. That breaks the "leaves never import composites" layering rule.
  - It is also a cycle waiting to happen. The later flow tasks will almost certainly rework `requests/put-in-sheet`, `take-out-sheet` and `ask-extra-sheet` to consume the flows kit (keypad, picture grid, shell). Once they do, `flows → requests → flows` is a circular import.
  - The nativewind styling breakage in `kit.test.tsx` is the visible symptom: merely importing the kit now loads the whole requests tree and its module-load side effects.
  - The `jest.mock("@/features/requests", …)` at `kit.test.tsx:23` hides this and makes the kit test lie about what the kit imports.
  - Fix, either one: (a) drop the `useRequests` import and have the `app/(child)/add.tsx` route compute `waiting` and pass it as a prop, which keeps `AddSheet` presentational and the barrel a pure kit; or (b) move `AddSheet` out of flows (for example a `features/add` module, or into `requests`, which owns asks) so the flows barrel stays leaf-only.
  - After the move, remove the mock from `kit.test.tsx`.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `add-sheet.test.tsx` word-budget test (the last case): the cap is raised to 36 to fit the copy, so the brief's 32 is not enforced. Also, `getAllByText(/.+/)` only counts rendered `Text` nodes and could miss copy that is not in a `Text`. Report this as a brief deviation for the controller to ratify, not as a silent change.
- `add-sheet.test.tsx` "shows the five moments": it asserts `getByRole("button", { name: "Save some" })` but only looks up the other four tiles by text. Role and name queries for "Give cash to Mum" would follow the brief and also verify the accessible name.
- `_layout.tsx` (child tabs): `tabBarButton: () => <PlusTabButton …/>` creates a new inline component per render. That is acceptable here, but hoisting the `onPress` with `useCallback` would avoid remounting the button on every guide-state change.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The feature matches the brief and the tests are meaningful. Importing the requests composite into the leaf flows barrel is a layering violation that will become a circular import once the request sheets use the flows kit, and the `kit.test.tsx` mock only hides it. Fix it by passing `waiting` as a prop or relocating `AddSheet`. The word-budget overrun is a minor deviation for the controller to decide on.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant for Task 6, including both carry-overs.
  - Every file the brief lists has its hunk: `goal-ring.tsx`, `milestone-stars.tsx`, `use-goal-moments.ts`, `useSavingPace` in `use-goal.ts`, the `useMarkCelebrated` cache write in `use-goal-mutations.ts`, and the barrel in `index.ts`. Both test files are present.
  - Every test the brief names exists. The extras are "stars animate in otherwise", "marking writes celebratedAt into the cached goal", "two mounts cheer once" and "is the frequency of a running schedule".
  - Carry-over A matches the three required strings: `weekly.ts:900-902` gives cur<0 "£X went out of Spend this week.", cur>0 "Spend grew £X this week." and cur=0 "Spend stayed the same this week." The core test covers all three and the jar-screen test is updated.
  - Carry-over B: `refreshAll` runs `Promise.all([onRefresh(), history.refetch()])` at `jar-screen.tsx:~459`, and the test reads both `getJars` and `listMovementsSince` again.
- Implementer flag 1, `findLatestSchedule` gaining `frequency`:
  - The `frequency` column exists in `database.types.ts:66`.
  - `asFrequency` is already used at `repository.ts:148`, so the same mapping is reused.
  - The only other consumers are `use-goal.ts` (`useAllowance` selects only `amountMinor`) and tests. `goals.test.tsx` mocks the function, so nothing else is affected. The change is additive and the data test is updated.
- Implementer flag 3, `useGoalMoments` not called from `GoalCard` or `GoalScreen`: the plan places those calls in Task 7 (`2026-11-06-mobile-kid-rework-4-jars.md:306-307`). Not required here, so not a gap.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - The "₹X to go · about N weeks" copy and `weeksToGoal` usage are Task 7 UI. The `useSavingPace` data side is in place.
  - Parts of the hook can only be confirmed by running the jest suite. The reported 61 passes were not re-run.

### Strengths
- `useGoalMoments` follows the brief closely and is easy to read.
  - Stored stars only ever go up (`Math.max(seen ?? 0, lit)`, `use-goal-moments.ts:284`).
  - `starToCheer(seen, lit)` handles a lowered target and falling stars. First sight and a storage failure both go quiet, and nothing throws.
  - Storage errors log only `error.name`, so no sensitive text reaches the logs.
- Handler state is read through a `latest` ref, so the effect fires only on `[goalId, lit, reached]`.
- `GoalRing` clamps overshoot and non-finite values (`goal-ring.tsx:59`). The a11y split (labelled progressbar versus hidden decoration) is correct and tested.
- `MilestoneStars` respects reduced motion and tests both branches.
- The `useMarkCelebrated` cache write is scoped to the same goal id, with no refetch, which matches the web behaviour.
- The latch in `useHomeStep` is a reasonable fix for the cache write removing the reached card. `chooseHomeStep` already ranks goal-reached first (`home-step.ts:22`), so the latch does not override a higher-priority step.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. `use-goal-moments.ts:278-280`: the `inFlight` guard drops an effect run instead of queueing it. If `lit` or `reached` changes while the first async run is still reading storage (a few ms), the new run is skipped and that star or reached moment is missed until the next change or remount. Cheap to fix: re-run after `finally`, or key `inFlight` on `goalId:lit:reached`.
2. `use-goal-moments.ts:305-311`: `mark.mutateAsync` is not awaited, so `inFlight` clears before the cache write lands. A second mount in that window (a `GoalCard` and `GoalScreen` navigation overlap) would see `reachedAt && !celebratedAt` and celebrate again. The window is small and the reached branch has no test for it. Awaiting the mark inside the `try`, or leaving the id in `inFlight` until it settles, would close it.
3. `use-goal-moments.ts:275` versus `home-step.ts:22`: Home treats the goal as reached when `progress.reached` is true, while the hook uses `goal.reachedAt`. The brief mandates `reachedAt`, and `goal_progress` sets it on read, so they should agree. Task 7 should confirm they stay in step.
4. `home-step-card.tsx:121-138`: the latch clears only when the goal id changes. If the target is raised after a celebration while Home stays mounted, the "goal reached" card stays visible although the goal is no longer reached. It resets on remount, and the case is rare.
5. `use-goal.ts:410-415`: `useSavingPace` repeats `useAllowance`'s query options (`queryFn`, key, enabled). It shares the cache correctly, but a shared `scheduleQuery` options helper would stop the two drifting apart.
6. Process: the implementer reports no RED run for Task 6 (report, deviation 4). The tests do assert real behaviour, so this is only a TDD process note.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The brief's interfaces, tests and both carry-overs are implemented correctly, and the additive `frequency` change checks out against the DB types and all callers. The remaining issues are small race and polish items, none blocking, and the `GoalCard`/`GoalScreen` wiring belongs to Task 7.

2. `use-goal-moments.ts:305-311`: `mark.mutateAsync` is not awaited, so `inFlight` clears before the cache write lands. A second mount in that window (a `GoalCard` and `GoalScreen` navigation overlap) would see `reachedAt && !celebratedAt` and celebrate again. The window is small and the reached branch has no test for it. Awaiting the mark inside the `try`, or leaving the id in `inFlight` until it settles, would close it.
3. `use-goal-moments.ts:275` versus `home-step.ts:22`: Home treats the goal as reached when `progress.reached` is true, while the hook uses `goal.reachedAt`. The brief mandates `reachedAt`, and `goal_progress` sets it on read, so they should agree. Task 7 should confirm they stay in step.
4. `home-step-card.tsx:121-138`: the latch clears only when the goal id changes. If the target is raised after a celebration while Home stays mounted, the "goal reached" card stays visible although the goal is no longer reached. It resets on remount, and the case is rare.
5. `use-goal.ts:410-415`: `useSavingPace` repeats `useAllowance`'s query options (`queryFn`, key, enabled). It shares the cache correctly, but a shared `scheduleQuery` options helper would stop the two drifting apart.
6. Process: the implementer reports no RED run for Task 6 (report, deviation 4). The tests do assert real behaviour, so this is only a TDD process note.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The brief's interfaces, tests and both carry-overs are implemented correctly, and the additive `frequency` change checks out against the DB types and all callers. The remaining issues are small race and polish items, none blocking, and the `GoalCard`/`GoalScreen` wiring belongs to Task 7.

timestamp: 2026-11-06T06:43:50.487Z
time: 2026-11-06 06:43
chars: 3623

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every listed file and interface is present. Both answers go through `useDailyCheckin`. Neither writes a ledger row. Nope calls `router.back()` and Yes calls `router.replace("/(child)/purchase")`, both without awaiting the write (`checkin-screen.tsx:26-29`). The `done` ref ignores a second tap. A failed write only calls `logger.warn`, with no error UI. The marker is written before the push (`use-checkin-prompt.ts:44-47`). The diff doesn't touch Home, `(child)/_layout.tsx` or `(tabs)/**`.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from diff: `useDailyCheckin` invalidating the progress query, and `progressCopy.streakDays(0)` reading as non-shaming, both live in unchanged code. Check both. Home also has to call `useCheckinPrompt`, which is another task.
- Deviation 3 (importing `familyToday` from `@/lib`) is fine under CLAUDE.md rule 18.

### Strengths
- Family-local days are used consistently. `checkinDecision` and `familyToday(timezone, now)` are called with the family timezone and never a device-local date.
- Double pushes are guarded properly. `decide` and the `askedDay.current = day` assignment run with no await between them (`use-checkin-prompt.ts:43-44`), so concurrent focus runs can't both push. The ref also covers a storage failure.
- `prompt-day.ts` never throws, and `run()` has a top-level `.catch` (`use-checkin-prompt.ts:49`).
- The hook tests cover real behaviour: real AsyncStorage, real query cache, fake time for family midnight, and storage failure.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
- `checkin-screen.test.tsx:57-62`: the "buttons are xl keycaps" test asserts `buttonVariants(...)` called directly in the test, not what the screen renders. It would still pass if the screen used `size="lg"`. The only screen-side assertion is `toBeOnTheScreen`. Fix: assert on the rendered button, for example its `style` height, or `toHaveStyle` if the host node carries it. Otherwise mock `Button` in this test and assert that `size="xl"` and the keycap variants are passed. The report acknowledges the className limitation, but the test still doesn't verify the requirement.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- `use-checkin-prompt.ts:41`: the `decision === "skip" && !current.data` condition also fires when the skip came from "already asked today" (`askedDay` or the stored day) while progress is unloaded. That means a network refetch on every Home focus all day for no reason. Fix: only refetch on this branch when the skip wasn't caused by the asked-day checks, or check `stored`/`askedDay` before refetching.
- `use-checkin-prompt.ts:42`: `decide(fresh.data, new Date())` uses "now" as `readAt` for fresh data, not `dataUpdatedAt`. This is correct for a just-completed fetch. If the refetch fails, `fresh.data` can still be the old stale data, and "now" as `readAt` would then treat it as fresh. The "refresh" decision can reach this path only when data exists. So after a failed refetch, stale `checkedInToday: false` from yesterday would show the sheet, even if the child already checked in today. Fix: use `fresh.isError ? skip : ...`, or use `progress.dataUpdatedAt` from the result.
- No test covers the "decision is skip because `askedDay` is set" path with progress loading, so the refetch-on-skip branch has no regression guard.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** Behaviour matches the brief and the hook is race-safe. The xl-keycap test doesn't exercise the screen, which is the Important issue to fix. The failed-refetch case showing a stale prompt (Minor, `use-checkin-prompt.ts:42`) is worth tightening too.

### Spec Compliance

- ✅ Spec compliant. Every file in the brief's list has its hunk, in the brief's order. `HomeHeader` → moments, or `HomeHero` when there are none → `PendingPurchases` → two `JarTile`s → `<GoalCard currency />` → `NextStepCard`. The bell goes to `/(child)/news`, per the controller ruling. `JarTile` is exported from `@/features/home` (`index.ts:2`) with `onPress` left unset. The list card, `JarGraph`, `PushPermissionCard`, `HomeStepCard` and the date overline are gone from `home-screen.tsx`.
- ✅ Leaf modules do not import composites, and I found no barrel cycle. `home/` imports only other features through their barrels, and `index.ts` adds only `jar-tile`. `GoalCard` and `NextStepCard` props are unchanged. The two new query keys (`query.ts`) share the `home` prefix.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - **Push prompt.** Removing `PushPermissionCard` from the child Home leaves no child surface that asks for push permission. The brief mandates the removal, but the controller should confirm the push-settings page, reached via Me, is the intended place. The ruling in the dispatch says `/notifications` is that page.
  - **`selectMoments` and Home's word count.** `selectMoments` can return up to `MAX_MOMENTS` cards, and I did not read that constant. Several moment cards would add about 6 to 8 words each. Home's 30-word limit is only tested with the hero showing, so the moments case is unmeasured.

### Strengths

- Pull-to-refresh works. `keys.home(userId)` is `["home", id]`, `useHome` is keyed on it (`use-jars.ts:16`), and `due` and `week` share the prefix, so one invalidation refetches all three. The "refetches on pull to refresh" test confirms it by asserting the jars query ran twice and the new balance rendered. Calling `refetch()` as well would have double-fetched.
- Every side read degrades quietly. Requests, due and progress fall back to null or defaults, and the `week` hook returns null, which `heroLine` turns into "Good to see you!". The "Home stays up when side reads fail" test covers all four failing at once.
- `JarTile` matches the brief. A `button` named `${name} ${amount}` only when `onPress` is set, otherwise an accessible group. It uses `numberOfLines={1}` and `adjustsFontSizeToFit`, and the tests cover both modes.
- `MomentCard` is two single-line `Text`s. The allowance-due card is not pressable. Decorative icons are hidden from the accessibility tree.
- `useHomeWeek` converts timestamps to local days with `localDate` and widens the UTC query window by a day, which is correct across timezones.
- Colours come from tokens, with no raw hex.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

- **Word-budget test is fragile.** `home-screen.test.tsx:912` pins `≤ 20` outside the mission and goal cards. The implementer measured exactly 20, so any change makes it fail. It counts "Hi Sam!" (2 words), both chip counts, the bubble, both tile names, amounts and captions.
  - A nickname of two or more words, or a longer hero string, breaks it. That is a test-data issue, because the real limit of 30 still holds.
  - `heroLine` returns 3 to 6 words, so only the "Good to see you!" path (4 words) is exercised here.
  - Suggested fix: assert against the real 30 limit, with the goal card and mission counted, or leave a few words of slack. Also add a case with one moment card showing, so the 30-word limit is checked on that path too.
- **Hero-to-moment layout jump.** `home-screen.tsx` renders `HomeHero` while `requests` and `due` are still loading, then swaps it for moment cards when they arrive. The 120 dp Jarso and the bubble flash before the moment appears. Consider holding the slot until requests and due have settled, or accept it as cosmetic.
- **Chip omitted from the accessibility label.** `moment-card.tsx:549` and `:558` label the card `${title} ${line}` only, so a screen reader never hears the gold chip text. Add the chip when present.
- **Week query key ignores `today`.** `use-home-extras.ts:30` keys the week query on `homeWeek(userId)` only, while its `queryFn` depends on `context.today`. If the app stays open past midnight without a refetch, the window is stale. Pull-to-refresh and the mutations' invalidations of `keys.home` fix it, so this is low severity.
- **Dead code left behind.** `JarGraph`, `JarsCard` and `HomeStepCard` are exported but no longer used by Home. `keys.balance` is now only used by `JarGraph`. `PushPermissionCard` is still used by `parent/family-home-screen.tsx`, so it stays. Delete the three unused components, or note them for the whole-branch cleanup.
- **TDD note.** The report says the tests were written after the components, with no RED run. The tests are behavioural and not mock-trivial, so I am not raising this as a defect.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief and the binding constraints, and Home data (`keys.home`) does refresh on pull-to-refresh. The only real weaknesses are a brittle word-budget test pinned at exactly 20 and a few small polish items, none of which block merge.
